class="ib-country-fn-alpha">
<li value="1">The official flag of Northern Ireland is the <a href="/wiki/Union_Jack" title="Union Jack">Union Jack</a>  <i><a href="/wiki/De_jure" title="De jure">de jure</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Ulster_Banner" title="Ulster Banner">Ulster Banner</a> was used by the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Northern_Ireland" title="Parliament of Northern Ireland">Parliament of Northern Ireland</a> from 1953 until the latter was abolished in 1973. The Ulster Banner is still used by some organisations and entities and used to represent Northern Ireland when it is playing as a national team. See <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland_flags_issue" title="Northern Ireland flags issue">Northern Ireland flags issue</a> for more.</li><li value="2"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1041539562">.mw-parser-output .citation{word-wrap:break-word}.mw-parser-output .citation:target{background-color:rgba(0,127,255,0.133)}</style><span class="citation wikicite" id="endnote_footnote_b"><b><a href="#ref_footnote_b">^</a></b></span>  Irish is an official language of Northern Ireland.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
English serves as a <i><a href="/wiki/De_facto" title="De facto">de facto</a></i> language of government and diplomacy. Irish and Ulster Scots both have commissioners for the languages<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> and are recognised minority languages of the UK.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup></li><li value="3"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1041539562"/><span class="citation wikicite" id="endnote_footnote_c"><b><a href="#ref_footnote_c">^</a></b></span>  +44 is always followed by 28 when calling landlines. The code is 028 within the UK and 048 from the Republic of Ireland where it is treated as a domestic call.</li>
</ol></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Northern_Ireland_-_Counties.png" class="image"><img alt="Northern Ireland – Counties" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/Northern_Ireland_-_Counties.png/220px-Northern_Ireland_-_Counties.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="187" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/Northern_Ireland_-_Counties.png/330px-Northern_Ireland_-_Counties.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/Northern_Ireland_-_Counties.png/440px-Northern_Ireland_-_Counties.png 2x" data-file-width="1677" data-file-height="1426" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Northern_Ireland_-_Counties.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The traditional counties of Northern Ireland</div></div></div>
<p><b>Northern Ireland</b> (<a href="/wiki/Irish_language" title="Irish language">Irish</a>: <i lang="ga">Tuaisceart Éireann</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="ga-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Irish" title="Help:IPA/Irish">[ˈt̪ˠuəʃcəɾˠt̪ˠ ˈeːɾʲən̪ˠ]</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:Tuaisceart_Eireann.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/c6/Tuaisceart_Eireann.ogg" class="internal" title="Tuaisceart Eireann.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span>;<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Ulster_Scots_dialect" title="Ulster Scots dialect">Ulster-Scots</a>: <i lang="sco">Norlin Airlann</i>) is a part of the <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">United Kingdom</a>, situated in the north-east of the island of <a href="/wiki/Ireland" title="Ireland">Ireland</a>, that is <a href="#Descriptions">variously described</a> as a country, province or region.<sup id="cite_ref-ONS_Countries_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ONS_Countries-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-alphabeticalNI_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-alphabeticalNI-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-interpretingNI_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-interpretingNI-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-placeApart_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-placeApart-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Northern Ireland shares <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Ireland%E2%80%93United_Kingdom_border" title="Republic of Ireland–United Kingdom border">an open border</a> to the south and west with the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Ireland" title="Republic of Ireland">Republic of Ireland</a>. In <a href="/wiki/2021_United_Kingdom_census" title="2021 United Kingdom census">2021</a>, its population was 1,903,100,<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> making up about 27% of <a href="/wiki/Ireland#Demographics" title="Ireland">Ireland's population</a> and about 3% of the <a href="/wiki/Demography_of_the_United_Kingdom#Population" title="Demography of the United Kingdom">UK's population</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland_Assembly" title="Northern Ireland Assembly">Northern Ireland Assembly</a>, established by the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland_Act_1998" title="Northern Ireland Act 1998">Northern Ireland Act 1998</a>, holds responsibility for a range of <a href="/wiki/Devolution" title="Devolution">devolved</a> policy matters, while other areas are reserved for the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Government of the United Kingdom">UK Government</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Northern_Ireland" title="Government of Northern Ireland">government of Northern Ireland</a> cooperates with the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_the_Republic_of_Ireland" class="mw-redirect" title="Government of the Republic of Ireland">government of the Republic of Ireland</a> in several areas agreed under the terms of the <a href="/wiki/Belfast_Agreement" class="mw-redirect" title="Belfast Agreement">Belfast Agreement</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-gfa_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gfa-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> The Republic of Ireland also has a consultative role on non-devolved governmental matters through the  British-Irish Governmental Conference (BIIG).<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Northern Ireland was created in May 1921, when <a href="/wiki/Partition_of_Ireland" title="Partition of Ireland">Ireland was partitioned</a> by the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Ireland_Act_1920" title="Government of Ireland Act 1920">Government of Ireland Act 1920</a>, creating a devolved government for the <a href="/wiki/Counties_of_Northern_Ireland" title="Counties of Northern Ireland">six northeastern counties</a>. As was intended, Northern Ireland had a <a href="/wiki/Unionism_in_Ireland" title="Unionism in Ireland">unionist</a> majority, who wanted to remain in the United Kingdom;<sup id="cite_ref-McKittrick-McVea_p5_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-McKittrick-McVea_p5-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> they were generally the <a href="/wiki/Ulster_Protestants" title="Ulster Protestants">Protestant</a> descendants of <a href="/wiki/Plantation_of_Ulster" title="Plantation of Ulster">colonists from Britain</a>. Meanwhile, the majority in <a href="/wiki/Southern_Ireland_(1921%E2%80%931922)" title="Southern Ireland (1921–1922)">Southern Ireland</a> (which became the <a href="/wiki/Irish_Free_State" title="Irish Free State">Irish Free State</a> in 1922), and a significant minority in Northern Ireland, were <a href="/wiki/Irish_nationalism" title="Irish nationalism">Irish nationalists</a> (generally <a href="/wiki/Irish_Catholics" title="Irish Catholics">Catholics</a>) who wanted a <a href="/wiki/United_Ireland" title="United Ireland">united independent Ireland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Today, the former generally see themselves as British and the latter generally see themselves as Irish, while a Northern Irish or <a href="/wiki/Ulster" title="Ulster">Ulster</a> identity is claimed by a significant minority from all backgrounds.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The creation of Northern Ireland was accompanied by violence both in defence of and against partition. During <a href="/wiki/The_Troubles_(1920%E2%80%931922)" class="mw-redirect" title="The Troubles (1920–1922)">the conflict of 1920–22</a>, the capital <a href="/wiki/Belfast" title="Belfast">Belfast</a> saw major <a href="/wiki/Communal_violence" title="Communal violence">communal violence</a>, mainly between Protestant unionist and Catholic nationalist civilians.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELynch201911,_100–101_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELynch201911,_100–101-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> More than 500 were killed<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELynch201999_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELynch201999-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> and more than 10,000 became refugees, mostly Catholics.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELynch2019171–176_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELynch2019171–176-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> For the next fifty years, Northern Ireland had an unbroken series of <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Northern_Ireland_(1921%E2%80%931972)" title="Government of Northern Ireland (1921–1972)">Unionist Party governments</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> There was informal mutual <a href="/wiki/Segregation_in_Northern_Ireland" title="Segregation in Northern Ireland">segregation</a> by both communities,<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> and the Unionist governments were accused of discrimination against the Irish nationalist and Catholic minority.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> In the late 1960s, a <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland_Civil_Rights_Association" title="Northern Ireland Civil Rights Association">campaign to end discrimination</a> against Catholics and nationalists was opposed by <a href="/wiki/Ulster_loyalism" title="Ulster loyalism">loyalists</a>, who saw it as a <a href="/wiki/Irish_republicanism" title="Irish republicanism">republican</a> front.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> This unrest sparked <a href="/wiki/The_Troubles" title="The Troubles">the Troubles</a>, a thirty-year conflict involving republican and loyalist paramilitaries and state forces, which claimed over 3,500 lives and injured 50,000 others.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto2_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto2-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> The 1998 <a href="/wiki/Good_Friday_Agreement" title="Good Friday Agreement">Good Friday Agreement</a> was a major step in the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland_peace_process" title="Northern Ireland peace process">peace process</a>, including <a href="/wiki/Decommissioning_in_Northern_Ireland" title="Decommissioning in Northern Ireland">paramilitary disarmament</a> and security normalisation, although <a href="/wiki/Sectarianism" title="Sectarianism">sectarianism</a> and segregation remain major social problems, and sporadic violence has continued.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Northern_Ireland" title="Economy of Northern Ireland">economy of Northern Ireland</a> was the most industrialised in Ireland at the time of partition, but soon began to decline, a decline exacerbated by the political and social turmoil of the Troubles.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> Its economy has grown significantly since the late 1990s. The initial growth came from the "<a href="/wiki/Peace_Dividend" class="mw-redirect" title="Peace Dividend">peace dividend</a>" and increased trade with the Republic of Ireland, continuing with a significant increase in tourism, investment, and business from around the world. Unemployment in Northern Ireland peaked at 17.2% in 1986, but dropped back down to below 10% in the 2010s,<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> similar to the rate of the rest of the UK.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Cultural links between Northern Ireland, the rest of Ireland, and the rest of the UK are complex, with Northern Ireland sharing both the <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Ireland" title="Culture of Ireland">culture of Ireland</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Culture of the United Kingdom">culture of the United Kingdom</a>. In many sports, <a href="/wiki/All-Ireland" title="All-Ireland">Ireland fields a single team</a>, with the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland_national_football_team" title="Northern Ireland national football team">Northern Ireland national football team</a> being an exception to this. Northern Ireland <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland_at_the_Commonwealth_Games" title="Northern Ireland at the Commonwealth Games">competes separately</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_Games" title="Commonwealth Games">Commonwealth Games</a>, and people from Northern Ireland may compete for either <a href="/wiki/Great_Britain_at_the_Olympics" title="Great Britain at the Olympics">Great Britain</a> or <a href="/wiki/Ireland_at_the_Olympics" title="Ireland at the Olympics">Ireland</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Olympic_Games" title="Olympic Games">Olympic Games</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Northern_Ireland" title="History of Northern Ireland">History of Northern Ireland</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Ireland" title="History of Ireland">History of Ireland</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ulster#History" title="Ulster">Ulster §&#160;History</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cannon_on_Derry_City_Walls_SMC_2007.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Cannon_on_Derry_City_Walls_SMC_2007.jpg/220px-Cannon_on_Derry_City_Walls_SMC_2007.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="139" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Cannon_on_Derry_City_Walls_SMC_2007.jpg/330px-Cannon_on_Derry_City_Walls_SMC_2007.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Cannon_on_Derry_City_Walls_SMC_2007.jpg/440px-Cannon_on_Derry_City_Walls_SMC_2007.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3099" data-file-height="1953" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cannon_on_Derry_City_Walls_SMC_2007.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Cannon on the <a href="/wiki/Derry_city_walls" title="Derry city walls">Derry city walls</a></div></div></div>
<p>The region that is now Northern Ireland was long inhabited by native <a href="/wiki/Gaels" title="Gaels">Gaels</a> who were Irish-speaking and predominantly Catholic.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> It was made up of several Gaelic kingdoms and territories and was part of the province of <a href="/wiki/Ulster" title="Ulster">Ulster</a>. In 1169, <a href="/wiki/Anglo-Norman_invasion_of_Ireland" title="Anglo-Norman invasion of Ireland">Ireland was invaded by a coalition</a> of forces under the command of the English crown that quickly overran and occupied most of the island, beginning 800 years of foreign central authority. Attempts at resistance were swiftly crushed everywhere outside of Ulster. Unlike in the rest of the country, where Gaelic authority continued only in scattered, remote pockets, the major kingdoms of Ulster would mostly remain intact with English authority in the province contained to <a href="/wiki/Earldom_of_Ulster" title="Earldom of Ulster">areas on the eastern coast</a> closest to Great Britain. English power gradually eroded in the face of stubborn Irish resistance in the centuries that followed; eventually being reduced to only the city of Dublin and its suburbs. When <a href="/wiki/Henry_VIII" title="Henry VIII">Henry VIII</a> launched the 16th century <a href="/wiki/Tudor_conquest_of_Ireland" title="Tudor conquest of Ireland">Tudor re-conquest of Ireland</a>, Ulster once resisted most effectively. In the <a href="/wiki/Nine_Years%27_War_(Ireland)" title="Nine Years&#39; War (Ireland)">Nine Years' War</a> (1594–1603), an alliance of Gaelic chieftains led by the two most powerful Ulster lords, <a href="/wiki/Hugh_Roe_O%27Donnell" title="Hugh Roe O&#39;Donnell">Hugh Roe O'Donnell</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Earl_of_Tyrone" title="Earl of Tyrone">Earl of Tyrone</a> fought against the <a href="/wiki/Dublin_Castle_administration" title="Dublin Castle administration">English government in Ireland</a>. The Ulster-dominated alliance represented the first Irish united front (prior resistance had always been localized). Despite being able to cement an alliance with Spain and major victories early on, inevitable defeat was virtually guaranteed following England's victory at the <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Kinsale" title="Siege of Kinsale">siege of Kinsale</a>. In 1607, the rebellion's leaders <a href="/wiki/Flight_of_the_Earls" title="Flight of the Earls">fled to mainland Europe</a> alongside much of Ulster's Gaelic nobility. Their lands were confiscated by <a href="/wiki/The_Crown" title="The Crown">the Crown</a> and colonized with English-speaking Protestant <a href="/wiki/Settler" title="Settler">settlers</a> from Britain, in the <a href="/wiki/Plantation_of_Ulster" title="Plantation of Ulster">Plantation of Ulster</a>. This led to the founding of many of Ulster's towns and created a lasting <a href="/wiki/Ulster_Protestants" title="Ulster Protestants">Ulster Protestant</a> community with ties to Britain. The <a href="/wiki/Irish_Rebellion_of_1641" title="Irish Rebellion of 1641">Irish Rebellion of 1641</a> began in Ulster. The rebels wanted an end to anti-Catholic discrimination, greater Irish self-governance, and to roll back the Plantation. It developed into an ethnic conflict between Irish Catholics and British Protestant settlers and became part of the wider <a href="/wiki/Wars_of_the_Three_Kingdoms" title="Wars of the Three Kingdoms">Wars of the Three Kingdoms</a> (1639–53), which ended with the <a href="/wiki/Cromwellian_conquest_of_Ireland" title="Cromwellian conquest of Ireland">English Parliamentarian conquest</a>. Further Protestant victories in the <a href="/wiki/Williamite_War_in_Ireland" title="Williamite War in Ireland">Williamite-Jacobite War</a> (1688–91) solidified <a href="/wiki/Church_of_Ireland" title="Church of Ireland">Anglican Protestant</a> rule in the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Ireland" title="Kingdom of Ireland">Kingdom of Ireland</a>. The Williamite victories of the <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Derry" title="Siege of Derry">siege of Derry</a> (1689) and <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Boyne" title="Battle of the Boyne">Battle of the Boyne</a> (1690) are still celebrated by some Protestants in Northern Ireland.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Many more Scots Protestants migrated to Ulster during the <a href="/wiki/Seven_ill_years" title="Seven ill years">Scottish famine of the 1690s</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Dollysbrae_Road_-_geograph.org.uk_-_100732.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Dollysbrae_Road_-_geograph.org.uk_-_100732.jpg/220px-Dollysbrae_Road_-_geograph.org.uk_-_100732.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Dollysbrae_Road_-_geograph.org.uk_-_100732.jpg/330px-Dollysbrae_Road_-_geograph.org.uk_-_100732.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Dollysbrae_Road_-_geograph.org.uk_-_100732.jpg/440px-Dollysbrae_Road_-_geograph.org.uk_-_100732.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="480" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Dollysbrae_Road_-_geograph.org.uk_-_100732.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Dolly's Brae, site of the "<a href="/wiki/Dolly%27s_Brae_conflict" title="Dolly&#39;s Brae conflict">Battle of Dolly's Brae</a>" (1849) between <a href="/wiki/Orange_Order" title="Orange Order">Orangemen</a> and Catholic <a href="/wiki/Ribbonmen" class="mw-redirect" title="Ribbonmen">Ribbonmen</a></div></div></div>
<p>Following the Williamite victory, and contrary to the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Limerick" title="Treaty of Limerick">Treaty of Limerick</a> (1691), a series of <a href="/wiki/Penal_Laws" title="Penal Laws">Penal Laws</a> were passed by the <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Ascendancy" title="Protestant Ascendancy">Anglican Protestant ruling class</a> in Ireland. The intention was to disadvantage Catholics and, to a lesser extent, <a href="/wiki/Presbyterianism" title="Presbyterianism">Presbyterians</a>. Some 250,000 Ulster Presbyterians emigrated to the <a href="/wiki/British_America" title="British America">British North American</a> colonies between 1717 and 1775.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> It is estimated that there are more than 27&#160;million <a href="/wiki/Scotch-Irish_Americans" title="Scotch-Irish Americans">Scotch-Irish Americans</a> now living in the United States,<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> along with many <a href="/wiki/Scotch-Irish_Canadians" title="Scotch-Irish Canadians">Scotch-Irish Canadians</a> in Canada. In the context of institutional discrimination, the 18th century saw secret, militant societies develop in Ulster and act on sectarian tensions in violent attacks. This escalated at the end of the century, especially during the County <a href="/wiki/Armagh_disturbances" title="Armagh disturbances">Armagh disturbances</a>, where the Protestant <a href="/wiki/Peep_o%27_Day_Boys" title="Peep o&#39; Day Boys">Peep o' Day Boys</a> fought the <a href="/wiki/Defenders_(Ireland)" title="Defenders (Ireland)">Catholic Defenders</a>. This led to the founding of the Protestant <a href="/wiki/Orange_Order" title="Orange Order">Orange Order</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Irish_Rebellion_of_1798" title="Irish Rebellion of 1798">Irish Rebellion of 1798</a> was led by the <a href="/wiki/Society_of_United_Irishmen" title="Society of United Irishmen">United Irishmen</a>; a cross-community <a href="/wiki/Irish_republicanism" title="Irish republicanism">Irish republican</a> group founded by Belfast Presbyterians, which sought Irish independence. Following this, the government of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Great_Britain" title="Kingdom of Great Britain">Kingdom of Great Britain</a> pushed for the two kingdoms to be merged, in an attempt to quell violent sectarianism, remove discriminatory laws, and prevent the spread of French-style republicanism. The <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_Great_Britain_and_Ireland" title="United Kingdom of Great Britain and Ireland">United Kingdom of Great Britain and Ireland</a> was formed in 1801 and governed from London. During the 19th century, legal reforms known as the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_emancipation" title="Catholic emancipation">Catholic emancipation</a> continued to remove discrimination against Catholics, and progressive programs enabled tenant farmers to buy land from landlords.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Home_Rule_Crisis">Home Rule Crisis</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Home_Rule_Crisis" title="Home Rule Crisis">Home Rule Crisis</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Carson_signing_Solemn_League_and_Covenant.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/d/de/Carson_signing_Solemn_League_and_Covenant.jpg/220px-Carson_signing_Solemn_League_and_Covenant.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="167" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/d/de/Carson_signing_Solemn_League_and_Covenant.jpg/330px-Carson_signing_Solemn_League_and_Covenant.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/d/de/Carson_signing_Solemn_League_and_Covenant.jpg 2x" data-file-width="412" data-file-height="312" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Carson_signing_Solemn_League_and_Covenant.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Signing of the <a href="/wiki/Ulster_Covenant" title="Ulster Covenant">Ulster Covenant</a> in 1912 in opposition to Home Rule</div></div></div>
<p>By the late 19th century, a large and disciplined cohort of <a href="/wiki/Irish_Parliamentary_Party" title="Irish Parliamentary Party">Irish Nationalist</a> MPs at <a href="/wiki/Westminster" title="Westminster">Westminster</a> committed the <a href="/wiki/Liberal_Party_(UK)" title="Liberal Party (UK)">Liberal Party</a> to <a href="/wiki/Irish_Home_Rule_movement" title="Irish Home Rule movement">"Irish Home Rule"</a>—self-government for Ireland, within the United Kingdom. This was bitterly opposed by <a href="/wiki/Unionism_in_Ireland" title="Unionism in Ireland">Irish Unionists</a>, most of whom were Protestants, who feared an Irish devolved government dominated by Irish nationalists and Catholics. The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Ireland_Bill_1886" title="Government of Ireland Bill 1886">Government of Ireland Bill 1886</a> and <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Ireland_Bill_1893" title="Government of Ireland Bill 1893">Government of Ireland Bill 1893</a> were defeated. However, Home Rule became a near-certainty in 1912 after the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Ireland_Act_1914" title="Government of Ireland Act 1914">Government of Ireland Act 1914</a> was first introduced. The Liberal government was dependent on Nationalist support, and the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_Act_1911" title="Parliament Act 1911">Parliament Act 1911</a> prevented the House of Lords from blocking the bill indefinitely.<sup id="cite_ref-Lydon326_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lydon326-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In response, unionists vowed to prevent Irish Home Rule, from <a href="/wiki/Conservative_Party_(UK)" title="Conservative Party (UK)">Conservative and Unionist Party</a> leaders such as <a href="/wiki/Bonar_Law" title="Bonar Law">Bonar Law</a> and Dublin-based barrister <a href="/wiki/Edward_Carson" title="Edward Carson">Edward Carson</a> to militant working class unionists in Ireland. This sparked the <a href="/wiki/Home_Rule_Crisis" title="Home Rule Crisis">Home Rule Crisis</a>. In September 1912, more than 500,000 Unionists signed the <a href="/wiki/Ulster_Covenant" title="Ulster Covenant">Ulster Covenant</a>, pledging to oppose Home Rule by any means and to defy any Irish government.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> In 1914, unionists <a href="/wiki/Larne_gun-running" title="Larne gun-running">smuggled thousands of rifles and rounds of ammunition</a> from <a href="/wiki/German_Empire" title="German Empire">Imperial Germany</a> for use by the <a href="/wiki/Ulster_Volunteers" title="Ulster Volunteers">Ulster Volunteers</a> (UVF), a paramilitary organisation formed to oppose Home Rule. Irish nationalists had also formed a paramilitary organisation, the <a href="/wiki/Irish_Volunteers" title="Irish Volunteers">Irish Volunteers</a>. It sought to ensure Home Rule was implemented, and it <a href="/wiki/Howth_gun-running" title="Howth gun-running">smuggled its own weapons into Ireland</a> a few months after the Ulster Volunteers.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Ireland seemed to be on the brink of civil war.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Unionists were in a minority in Ireland as a whole, but a majority in the province of <a href="/wiki/Ulster" title="Ulster">Ulster</a>, especially the counties <a href="/wiki/County_Antrim" title="County Antrim">Antrim</a>, <a href="/wiki/County_Down" title="County Down">Down</a>, <a href="/wiki/County_Armagh" title="County Armagh">Armagh</a> and <a href="/wiki/County_Londonderry" title="County Londonderry">Londonderry</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-StephenGwynn_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-StephenGwynn-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Unionists argued that if Home Rule could not be stopped then all or part of Ulster should be excluded from it.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> In May 1914, the UK Government introduced an Amending Bill to allow for 'Ulster' to be excluded from Home Rule. There was then debate over how much of Ulster should be excluded and for how long. Some Ulster unionists were willing to tolerate the 'loss' of some mainly-Catholic areas of the province.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> The crisis was interrupted by the outbreak of the <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">First World War</a> in August 1914, and <a href="/wiki/Ireland_and_World_War_I" title="Ireland and World War I">Ireland's involvement in it</a>. The UK Government abandoned the Amending Bill, and instead rushed through a new bill, the <a href="/wiki/Suspensory_Act_1914" title="Suspensory Act 1914">Suspensory Act 1914</a>, suspending Home Rule for the duration of the war,<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> with the exclusion of Ulster still to be decided.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Partition_of_Ireland">Partition of Ireland</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Partition_of_Ireland" title="Partition of Ireland">Partition of Ireland</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Irish_UK_election_1918.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/Irish_UK_election_1918.png/220px-Irish_UK_election_1918.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="273" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/Irish_UK_election_1918.png/330px-Irish_UK_election_1918.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/Irish_UK_election_1918.png/440px-Irish_UK_election_1918.png 2x" data-file-width="834" data-file-height="1034" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Irish_UK_election_1918.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Result of the <a href="/wiki/1918_Irish_general_election" title="1918 Irish general election">1918 general election in Ireland</a></div></div></div>
<p>By the end of the war (during which the 1916 <a href="/wiki/Easter_Rising" title="Easter Rising">Easter Rising</a> had taken place), most Irish nationalists now wanted full independence rather than home rule. In September 1919, British Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/David_Lloyd_George" title="David Lloyd George">David Lloyd George</a> tasked a committee with planning another home rule bill. Headed by <a href="/wiki/Unionism_in_Ireland" title="Unionism in Ireland">English unionist</a> politician <a href="/wiki/Walter_Long,_1st_Viscount_Long" title="Walter Long, 1st Viscount Long">Walter Long</a>, it was known as the 'Long Committee'. It decided that two devolved governments should be established—one for the nine counties of Ulster and one for the rest of Ireland—together with a <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Ireland" title="Council of Ireland">Council of Ireland</a> for the "encouragement of Irish unity".<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> Most Ulster unionists wanted the territory of the Ulster government to be reduced to six counties so that it would have a larger Protestant unionist majority. They feared that the territory would not last if it included too many Catholics and Irish nationalists. The six counties of <a href="/wiki/County_Antrim" title="County Antrim">Antrim</a>, <a href="/wiki/County_Down" title="County Down">Down</a>, <a href="/wiki/County_Armagh" title="County Armagh">Armagh</a>, <a href="/wiki/County_Londonderry" title="County Londonderry">Londonderry</a>, <a href="/wiki/County_Tyrone" title="County Tyrone">Tyrone</a> and <a href="/wiki/County_Fermanagh" title="County Fermanagh">Fermanagh</a> comprised the maximum area unionists believed they could dominate.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> The area that was to become Northern Ireland amounted to six of the nine counties of Ulster, in spite of the fact that in the last all Ireland election (<a href="/wiki/1918_Irish_general_election" title="1918 Irish general election">1918 Irish general election</a>) counties Fermanagh and Tyrone had Sinn Fein/Nationalist Party (Irish Parliamentary Party) majorities.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Events overtook the government. In the 1918 Irish general election, the pro-independence <a href="/wiki/Sinn_F%C3%A9in" title="Sinn Féin">Sinn Féin</a> party won the overwhelming majority of Irish seats. Sinn Féin's elected members boycotted the British parliament and founded a separate Irish parliament (<a href="/wiki/First_D%C3%A1il" title="First Dáil">Dáil Éireann</a>), <a href="/wiki/Irish_Declaration_of_Independence" title="Irish Declaration of Independence">declaring the independent Irish Republic</a> covering the whole island. Many <a href="/wiki/Irish_republicans" class="mw-redirect" title="Irish republicans">Irish republicans</a> blamed the British establishment for the sectarian divisions in Ireland, and believed that Ulster Unionist defiance would fade once British rule was ended.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELynch201951–52_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELynch201951–52-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> The British authorities outlawed the Dáil in September 1919,<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> and a guerrilla conflict developed as the <a href="/wiki/Irish_Republican_Army_(1919%E2%80%931922)" title="Irish Republican Army (1919–1922)">Irish Republican Army</a> (IRA) began attacking British forces. This became known as the <a href="/wiki/Irish_War_of_Independence" title="Irish War of Independence">Irish War of Independence</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ulster_Welcomes_Her_King_%26_Queen_(10990906846).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Ulster_Welcomes_Her_King_%26_Queen_%2810990906846%29.jpg/220px-Ulster_Welcomes_Her_King_%26_Queen_%2810990906846%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="160" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Ulster_Welcomes_Her_King_%26_Queen_%2810990906846%29.jpg/330px-Ulster_Welcomes_Her_King_%26_Queen_%2810990906846%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Ulster_Welcomes_Her_King_%26_Queen_%2810990906846%29.jpg/440px-Ulster_Welcomes_Her_King_%26_Queen_%2810990906846%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2500" data-file-height="1814" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ulster_Welcomes_Her_King_%26_Queen_(10990906846).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Crowds in Belfast for the state opening of the Northern Ireland Parliament on 22 June 1921</div></div></div>
<p>Meanwhile, the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Ireland_Act_1920" title="Government of Ireland Act 1920">Government of Ireland Act 1920</a> passed through the British parliament in 1920. It would divide Ireland into two self-governing UK territories: the six northeastern counties (Northern Ireland) being ruled from <a href="/wiki/Belfast" title="Belfast">Belfast</a>, and the other twenty-six counties (Southern Ireland) being ruled from <a href="/wiki/Dublin" title="Dublin">Dublin</a>. Both would have a shared <a href="/wiki/Lord_Lieutenant_of_Ireland" title="Lord Lieutenant of Ireland">Lord Lieutenant of Ireland</a>, who would appoint both governments and a <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Ireland" title="Council of Ireland">Council of Ireland</a>, which the UK Government intended to evolve into an all-Ireland parliament.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> The Act received <a href="/wiki/Royal_assent" title="Royal assent">royal assent</a> that December, becoming the Government of Ireland Act 1920. It came into force on 3 May 1921,<sup id="cite_ref-O&#39;Day_299_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-O&#39;Day_299-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> partitioning Ireland and creating Northern Ireland. <a href="/wiki/1921_Irish_elections" title="1921 Irish elections">Elections to the Northern parliament</a> were held on 24 May, in which Unionists won most seats. Its parliament first met on 7 June and formed its <a href="/wiki/Craigavon_ministry" title="Craigavon ministry">first devolved government</a>, headed by Unionist Party leader <a href="/wiki/James_Craig,_1st_Viscount_Craigavon" title="James Craig, 1st Viscount Craigavon">James Craig</a>. Republican and nationalist members refused to attend. King George V addressed the ceremonial opening of the Northern parliament on 22 June.<sup id="cite_ref-O&#39;Day_299_54-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-O&#39;Day_299-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During 1920–22, in what became Northern Ireland, partition was accompanied by violence "in defence or opposition to the new settlement"<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELynch201911,_100–101_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELynch201911,_100–101-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> – see <a href="/wiki/The_Troubles_in_Northern_Ireland_(1920%E2%80%931922)" title="The Troubles in Northern Ireland (1920–1922)">The Troubles in Northern Ireland (1920–1922)</a>. The IRA carried out attacks on British forces in the north-east but was less active than in the south of Ireland. Protestant loyalists attacked the Catholic community in reprisal for IRA actions. In the summer of 1920, sectarian violence erupted in Belfast and Derry, and there were mass burnings of Catholic property in <a href="/wiki/Lisburn" title="Lisburn">Lisburn</a> and <a href="/wiki/Banbridge" title="Banbridge">Banbridge</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELynch201990–92_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELynch201990–92-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Conflict continued intermittently for two years, mostly in <a href="/wiki/Belfast" title="Belfast">Belfast</a>, which saw "savage and unprecedented" <a href="/wiki/Communal_violence" title="Communal violence">communal violence</a> between Protestant and Catholic civilians. There was rioting, gun battles, and bombings. Homes, businesses, and churches were attacked and people were expelled from workplaces and mixed neighbourhoods.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELynch201911,_100–101_21-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELynch201911,_100–101-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> More than 500 were killed<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELynch201999_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELynch201999-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> and more than 10,000 became refugees, most of them Catholics.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELynch201971–76_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELynch201971–76-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/British_Army" title="British Army">British Army</a> was deployed and the <a href="/wiki/Ulster_Special_Constabulary" title="Ulster Special Constabulary">Ulster Special Constabulary</a> (USC) was formed to help the regular police. The USC was almost wholly Protestant and some of its members carried out reprisal attacks on Catholics.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> A truce between British forces and the IRA was established on 11 July 1921, ending the fighting in most of Ireland. However, communal violence continued in Belfast, and in 1922 the IRA launched a guerrilla offensive in border areas of Northern Ireland.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Anglo-Irish_Treaty" title="Anglo-Irish Treaty">Anglo-Irish Treaty</a> was signed between representatives of the UK Government and the <a href="/wiki/Irish_Republic" title="Irish Republic">Irish Republic</a> on 6 December 1921. This created the <a href="/wiki/Irish_Free_State" title="Irish Free State">Irish Free State</a>. Under the terms of the treaty, Northern Ireland would become part of the Free State unless the government opted out by presenting an address to the king, although in practice partition remained in place.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Coat_of_Arms_of_Northern_Ireland.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Coat_of_Arms_of_Northern_Ireland.svg/220px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Northern_Ireland.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="154" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Coat_of_Arms_of_Northern_Ireland.svg/330px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Northern_Ireland.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Coat_of_Arms_of_Northern_Ireland.svg/440px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Northern_Ireland.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="698" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Coat_of_Arms_of_Northern_Ireland.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Northern_Ireland" title="Coat of arms of Northern Ireland">Coat of arms of Northern Ireland</a> used between 1924 and 1973</div></div></div><p>
As expected, the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Northern_Ireland" title="Parliament of Northern Ireland">Parliament of Northern Ireland</a> resolved on 7 December 1922 (the day after the establishment of the Irish Free State) to exercise its right to opt out of the Free State by making an address to King <a href="/wiki/George_V" title="George V">George V</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> The text of the address was: <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style></p><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Most Gracious Sovereign, We, your Majesty's most dutiful and loyal subjects, the Senators and Commons of Northern Ireland in Parliament assembled, having learnt of the passing of the <a href="/wiki/Irish_Free_State_Constitution_Act_1922" title="Irish Free State Constitution Act 1922">Irish Free State Constitution Act 1922</a>, being the Act of Parliament for the ratification of the Articles of Agreement for a Treaty between Great Britain and Ireland, do, by this humble Address, pray your Majesty that the powers of the Parliament and Government of the Irish Free State shall no longer extend to Northern Ireland.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote><p> Shortly afterwards, the <a href="/wiki/Irish_Boundary_Commission" title="Irish Boundary Commission">Irish Boundary Commission</a> was established to decide on the border between the Irish Free State and Northern Ireland. Owing to the outbreak of the <a href="/wiki/Irish_Civil_War" title="Irish Civil War">Irish Civil War</a>, the work of the commission was delayed until 1925. The Free State government and <a href="/wiki/Irish_nationalism" title="Irish nationalism">Irish nationalists</a> hoped for a large transfer of territory to the Free State, as many border areas had nationalist majorities, leaving the remaining Northern Ireland too small to be viable.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> However, the commission's final report recommended only small transfers of territory, and in both directions. The Free State, Northern Ireland, and UK governments agreed to suppress the report and accept the <i>status quo</i>, while the UK Government agreed that the Free State would no longer have to pay its share of the UK national debt.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><h3><span id="1925.E2.80.931965"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="1925–1965">1925–1965</span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output
