<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Northern_Ireland" title="Economy of Northern Ireland">Economy of Northern Ireland</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:%27Titanic_Quarter%27_development,_Abercorn_Basin,_Belfast_-_geograph.org.uk_-_1378153.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/%27Titanic_Quarter%27_development%2C_Abercorn_Basin%2C_Belfast_-_geograph.org.uk_-_1378153.jpg/220px-%27Titanic_Quarter%27_development%2C_Abercorn_Basin%2C_Belfast_-_geograph.org.uk_-_1378153.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="161" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/%27Titanic_Quarter%27_development%2C_Abercorn_Basin%2C_Belfast_-_geograph.org.uk_-_1378153.jpg/330px-%27Titanic_Quarter%27_development%2C_Abercorn_Basin%2C_Belfast_-_geograph.org.uk_-_1378153.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/%27Titanic_Quarter%27_development%2C_Abercorn_Basin%2C_Belfast_-_geograph.org.uk_-_1378153.jpg/440px-%27Titanic_Quarter%27_development%2C_Abercorn_Basin%2C_Belfast_-_geograph.org.uk_-_1378153.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="468" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:%27Titanic_Quarter%27_development,_Abercorn_Basin,_Belfast_-_geograph.org.uk_-_1378153.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Goliath crane of <a href="/wiki/Harland_%26_Wolff" title="Harland &amp; Wolff">Harland &amp; Wolff</a> in <a href="/wiki/Belfast" title="Belfast">Belfast</a></div></div></div>
<p>Northern Ireland has traditionally had an industrial economy, most notably in shipbuilding, rope manufacture, and textiles, but the heaviest industry has since been replaced by services, primarily the public sector.
</p><p>Seventy percent of the economy's revenue comes from the service sector. Apart from the public sector, another important service sector is tourism, which rose to account for over 1% of the economy's revenue in 2004. Tourism has been a major growth area since the end of the Troubles. Key tourism attractions include the historic cities of Derry, Belfast, and <a href="/wiki/Armagh" title="Armagh">Armagh</a> and the many castles in Northern Ireland.
</p><p>The local economy has seen contraction during the <a href="/wiki/Great_Recession" title="Great Recession">Great Recession</a>. The Executive wishes to gain taxation powers from London, to align Northern Ireland's <a href="/wiki/Corporation_tax" class="mw-redirect" title="Corporation tax">corporation tax</a> rate with that of the Republic of Ireland.
</p><p>As in all of the UK, the economy of Northern Ireland was negatively impacted by the lockdowns and travel restrictions necessitated by the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic" title="COVID-19 pandemic">COVID-19 pandemic</a>. The tourism and hospitality industry was particularly hard hit. These sectors "have been mandated to close since 26 December 2020, with a very limited number of exceptions" and many restrictions were continuing into April 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> Hotels and other accommodations, for example, "closed apart from only for work-related stays".<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> Some restrictions were expected to be loosened in mid April but tourism was expected to remain very limited.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Northern_Ireland" class="mw-redirect" title="Transport in Northern Ireland">Transport in Northern Ireland</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Approaching_Seahill_station_-_geograph.org.uk_-_243422.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Approaching_Seahill_station_-_geograph.org.uk_-_243422.jpg/220px-Approaching_Seahill_station_-_geograph.org.uk_-_243422.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Approaching_Seahill_station_-_geograph.org.uk_-_243422.jpg/330px-Approaching_Seahill_station_-_geograph.org.uk_-_243422.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Approaching_Seahill_station_-_geograph.org.uk_-_243422.jpg/440px-Approaching_Seahill_station_-_geograph.org.uk_-_243422.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="426" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Approaching_Seahill_station_-_geograph.org.uk_-_243422.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>An <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland_Railways" class="mw-redirect" title="Northern Ireland Railways">NIR</a> <a href="/wiki/NIR_Class_3000" title="NIR Class 3000">C3K</a> <a href="/wiki/Railcar" title="Railcar">railcar</a></div></div></div>
<p>Northern Ireland has underdeveloped <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Northern_Ireland" class="mw-redirect" title="Transport in Northern Ireland">transport infrastructure</a>, with most infrastructure concentrated around Greater Belfast, Greater Derry, and Craigavon.  Northern Ireland is served by three airports – <a href="/wiki/Belfast_International_Airport" title="Belfast International Airport">Belfast International</a> near <a href="/wiki/Antrim,_County_Antrim" title="Antrim, County Antrim">Antrim</a>, <a href="/wiki/George_Best_Belfast_City_Airport" title="George Best Belfast City Airport">George Best Belfast City</a> integrated into the railway network at <a href="/wiki/Sydenham_railway_station,_Northern_Ireland" class="mw-redirect" title="Sydenham railway station, Northern Ireland">Sydenham</a> in East Belfast, and <a href="/wiki/City_of_Derry_Airport" title="City of Derry Airport">City of Derry</a> in County Londonderry.
</p><p>Major seaports at <a href="/wiki/Larne" title="Larne">Larne</a> and <a href="/wiki/Port_of_Belfast" class="mw-redirect" title="Port of Belfast">Belfast</a> carry passengers and freight between Great Britain and Northern Ireland.
</p><p>Passenger railways are operated by <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland_Railways" class="mw-redirect" title="Northern Ireland Railways">Northern Ireland Railways</a>. With <a href="/wiki/Iarnr%C3%B3d_%C3%89ireann" title="Iarnród Éireann">Iarnród Éireann</a> (Irish Rail), <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland_Railways" class="mw-redirect" title="Northern Ireland Railways">Northern Ireland Railways</a> co-operates in providing the joint <a href="/wiki/Enterprise_(train)" class="mw-redirect" title="Enterprise (train)">Enterprise</a> service between <a href="/wiki/Dublin_Connolly" class="mw-redirect" title="Dublin Connolly">Dublin Connolly</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lanyon_Place_railway_station" title="Lanyon Place railway station">Lanyon Place</a>. The whole of Ireland has a mainline railway network with a <a href="/wiki/Track_gauge_in_Ireland" title="Track gauge in Ireland">gauge of 5&#160;ft 3&#160;in (1,600&#160;mm)</a>, which is unique in Europe and has resulted in distinct rolling stock designs. The only preserved line of this gauge on the island is the <a href="/wiki/Downpatrick_and_County_Down_Railway" title="Downpatrick and County Down Railway">Downpatrick and County Down Railway</a>, which operates heritage steam and diesel locomotives. Main railway lines linking to and from <a href="/wiki/Belfast_Great_Victoria_Street_railway_station" title="Belfast Great Victoria Street railway station">Belfast Great Victoria Street railway station</a> and Lanyon Place railway station are:
</p>
<ul><li>The <a href="/wiki/Belfast%E2%80%93Derry_railway_line" class="mw-redirect" title="Belfast–Derry railway line">Derry Line</a> and the Portrush Branch.</li>
<li>The Larne Line</li>
<li>The Bangor Line</li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Portadown_railway_station" title="Portadown railway station">Portadown</a> Line</li></ul>
<p>Main motorways are:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/M1_motorway_(Northern_Ireland)" title="M1 motorway (Northern Ireland)">M1</a> connecting Belfast to the south and west, ending in <a href="/wiki/Dungannon" title="Dungannon">Dungannon</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/M2_motorway_(Northern_Ireland)" title="M2 motorway (Northern Ireland)">M2</a> connecting Belfast to the north.  An unconnected section of the <a href="/wiki/M2_motorway_(Northern_Ireland)" title="M2 motorway (Northern Ireland)">M2</a> also by-passes <a href="/wiki/Ballymena" title="Ballymena">Ballymena</a></li></ul>
<p>Additional short motorway spurs include:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/M12_motorway_(Northern_Ireland)" class="mw-redirect" title="M12 motorway (Northern Ireland)">M12</a> connecting the <a href="/wiki/M1_motorway_(Northern_Ireland)" title="M1 motorway (Northern Ireland)">M1</a> to <a href="/wiki/Portadown" title="Portadown">Portadown</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/M22_motorway_(Northern_Ireland)" title="M22 motorway (Northern Ireland)">M22</a> connecting the <a href="/wiki/M2_motorway_(Northern_Ireland)" title="M2 motorway (Northern Ireland)">M2</a> to near <a href="/wiki/Randalstown" title="Randalstown">Randalstown</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/M3_motorway_(Northern_Ireland)" title="M3 motorway (Northern Ireland)">M3</a> connecting the M1 (via the <a href="/wiki/Westlink_(road)" title="Westlink (road)">A12</a>) and M2 in Belfast with the A2 dual carriageway to <a href="/wiki/Bangor,_County_Down" title="Bangor, County Down">Bangor</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/M5_motorway_(Northern_Ireland)" title="M5 motorway (Northern Ireland)">M5</a> connecting Belfast to <a href="/wiki/Newtownabbey" title="Newtownabbey">Newtownabbey</a></li></ul>
<p>The cross-border road connecting the ports of <a href="/wiki/Larne" title="Larne">Larne</a> in Northern Ireland and <a href="/wiki/Rosslare_Harbour" title="Rosslare Harbour">Rosslare Harbour</a> in the Republic of Ireland is being upgraded as part of an EU-funded scheme. <a href="/wiki/European_route_E01" class="mw-redirect" title="European route E01">European route E01</a> runs from Larne through the island of Ireland, Spain, and Portugal to <a href="/wiki/Seville" title="Seville">Seville</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Demography_of_Northern_Ireland" title="Demography of Northern Ireland">Demography of Northern Ireland</a> and <a href="/wiki/People_of_Northern_Ireland" title="People of Northern Ireland">People of Northern Ireland</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Northern_Ireland_%E2%80%93_religion_or_religion_brought_up_in_(Christianity;_2011).png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/Northern_Ireland_%E2%80%93_religion_or_religion_brought_up_in_%28Christianity%3B_2011%29.png/220px-Northern_Ireland_%E2%80%93_religion_or_religion_brought_up_in_%28Christianity%3B_2011%29.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="171" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/Northern_Ireland_%E2%80%93_religion_or_religion_brought_up_in_%28Christianity%3B_2011%29.png/330px-Northern_Ireland_%E2%80%93_religion_or_religion_brought_up_in_%28Christianity%3B_2011%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/Northern_Ireland_%E2%80%93_religion_or_religion_brought_up_in_%28Christianity%3B_2011%29.png/440px-Northern_Ireland_%E2%80%93_religion_or_religion_brought_up_in_%28Christianity%3B_2011%29.png 2x" data-file-width="19059" data-file-height="14839" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Northern_Ireland_%E2%80%93_religion_or_religion_brought_up_in_(Christianity;_2011).png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>2011 census: differences in proportions of those who are, or were brought up, either Catholic or Protestant/Other Christians</div></div></div>
<p>The population of Northern Ireland has risen yearly since 1978. The population at the time of the 2021 census was 1.9&#160;million, having grown 5% over the previous decade.<sup id="cite_ref-2021_census_main_points_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2021_census_main_points-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> The population in 2011 was 1.8&#160;million, a rise of 7.5% over the previous decade.<sup id="cite_ref-Census_2011_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Census_2011-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> The current population makes up 2.8% of the UK's population (67&#160;million) and 27% of the island of Ireland's population (7.03&#160;million). The population density is 135 inhabitants / km<sup>2</sup>.
</p><p>As of the 2021 census, the population of Northern Ireland is almost entirely <a href="/wiki/White_people" title="White people">white</a> (96.6%).<sup id="cite_ref-Ethnic_group_census_2022_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ethnic_group_census_2022-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> In 2021, 86.5% of the population were born in Northern Ireland, with 4.8% born in Great Britain, 2.1% born in the Republic of Ireland, and 6.5% born elsewhere (more than half of them in another European country).<sup id="cite_ref-Country_of_birth_census_2022_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Country_of_birth_census_2022-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> In 2021 the largest non-white ethnic groups were <a href="/wiki/Black_people_in_Ireland" title="Black people in Ireland">black</a> (0.6%), <a href="/wiki/Non-resident_Indian_and_person_of_Indian_origin" class="mw-redirect" title="Non-resident Indian and person of Indian origin">Indian</a> (0.5%), and <a href="/wiki/Overseas_Chinese" title="Overseas Chinese">Chinese</a> (0.5%).<sup id="cite_ref-Ethnic_group_census_2022_149-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ethnic_group_census_2022-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, 88.8% of the population were born in Northern Ireland, 4.5% in Great Britain, and 2.9% in the Republic of Ireland. 4.3% were born elsewhere; triple the amount there were in 2001.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Northern_Ireland" title="Religion in Northern Ireland">Religion in Northern Ireland</a></div>
<p>At the 2021 census, 42.3% of the population identified as <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholicism_in_Ireland" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholicism in Ireland">Roman Catholic</a>, 37.3% as Protestant/other Christian, 1.3% as other religions, while 17.4% identified with no religion or did not state one.<sup id="cite_ref-2021_religion_152-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2021_religion-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> The biggest of the Protestant/other Christian denominations were the <a href="/wiki/Presbyterian_Church_in_Ireland" title="Presbyterian Church in Ireland">Presbyterian Church</a> (16.6%), the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_Ireland" title="Church of Ireland">Church of Ireland</a> (11.5%) and the <a href="/wiki/Methodist_Church_in_Ireland" title="Methodist Church in Ireland">Methodist Church</a> (2.3%).<sup id="cite_ref-2021_religion_152-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2021_religion-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> At the 2011 census, 41.5% of the population identified as Protestant/other Christian, 41% as Roman Catholic, 0.8% as other religions, while 17% identified with no religion or did not state one.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_153-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> In terms of background (i.e. religion or religion brought up in), at the 2021 census 45.7% of the population came from a Catholic background, 43.5% from a Protestant background, 1.5% from other religious backgrounds, and 5.6% from non-religious backgrounds.<sup id="cite_ref-2021_religion_152-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2021_religion-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> This was the first time since Northern Ireland's creation that there were more people from a Catholic background than Protestant.<sup id="cite_ref-first_catholic_majority_154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-first_catholic_majority-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> At the 2011 census, 48% came from a Protestant background, 45% from a Catholic background, 0.9% from other religious backgrounds, and 5.6% from non-religious backgrounds.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_153-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="barbox tright" style="overflow-x: auto;">

</div>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Identity_and_citizenship">Identity and citizenship</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/British_nationality_law" title="British nationality law">British nationality law</a> and <a href="/wiki/Irish_nationality_law" title="Irish nationality law">Irish nationality law</a></div>
<p>In Northern Ireland censuses, respondents can choose more than one national identity. In 2021:<sup id="cite_ref-2021_national_identity_157-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2021_national_identity-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>42.8% identified as British, solely or along with other national identities</li>
<li>33.3% identified as Irish, solely or along with other national identities</li>
<li>31.5% identified as Northern Irish, solely or along with other national identities</li></ul>
<p>The main national identities given in recent censuses were:
</p>
<table class="wikitable sortable" style="text-align:right">

<tbody><tr>
<th colspan="4">National identity of Northern Ireland residents<sup id="cite_ref-2011_Census_data_156-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2011_Census_data-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-2021_national_identity_157-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2021_national_identity-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup>
</th></tr>

<td>6.0%
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Several studies and surveys carried out between 1971 and 2006 have indicated that, in general, most Protestants in Northern Ireland see themselves primarily as British, whereas most Catholics see themselves primarily as Irish.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> This does not, however, account for the <a href="/wiki/People_of_Northern_Ireland" title="People of Northern Ireland">complex identities within Northern Ireland</a>, given that many of the population regard themselves as "Ulster" or "Northern Irish", either as a primary or secondary identity.
</p><p>A 2008 survey found that 57% of Protestants described themselves as British, while 32% identified as Northern Irish, 6% as Ulster, and 4% as Irish. Compared to a similar survey in 1998, this shows a fall in the percentage of Protestants identifying as British and Ulster and a rise in those identifying as Northern Irish. The 2008 survey found that 61% of Catholics described themselves as Irish, with 25% identifying as Northern Irish, 8% as British, and 1% as Ulster. These figures were largely unchanged from the 1998 results.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>People born in Northern Ireland are, with some exceptions, deemed by UK law to be <a href="/wiki/British_nationality_law" title="British nationality law">citizens of the United Kingdom</a>. They are also, with similar exceptions, entitled to be <a href="/wiki/Irish_nationality_law" title="Irish nationality law">citizens of Ireland</a>. This entitlement was reaffirmed in the 1998 <a href="/wiki/Good_Friday_Agreement" title="Good Friday Agreement">Good Friday Agreement</a> between the British and Irish governments, which provides that:
</p>
<blockquote><p>...it is the birthright of all the people of Northern Ireland to identify themselves and be accepted as Irish or British, or both, as they may so choose, and accordingly [the two governments] confirm that their right to hold both British and Irish citizenship is accepted by both Governments and would not be affected by any future change in the status of Northern Ireland.</p></blockquote>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Predominant_passport_held_northern_ireland.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Predominant_passport_held_northern_ireland.png/220px-Predominant_passport_held_northern_ireland.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="171" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Predominant_passport_held_northern_ireland.png/330px-Predominant_passport_held_northern_ireland.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Predominant_passport_held_northern_ireland.png/440px-Predominant_passport_held_northern_ireland.png 2x" data-file-width="18996" data-file-height="14790" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Predominant_passport_held_northern_ireland.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>2011: Map of most commonly held passport</div></div></div>
<p>As a result of the Agreement, the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Ireland" title="Constitution of Ireland">Constitution of the Republic of Ireland</a> was amended. The current wording provides that people born in Northern Ireland are entitled to be Irish citizens on the same basis as people from any other part of the island.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Neither government, however, extends its citizenship to all persons born in Northern Ireland. Both governments exclude some people born in Northern Ireland, in particular persons born without one parent who is a British or Irish citizen. The Irish restriction was given effect by the <a href="/wiki/Twenty-seventh_Amendment_of_the_Constitution_of_Ireland" title="Twenty-seventh Amendment of the Constitution of Ireland">twenty-seventh amendment to the Irish Constitution</a> in 2004. The position in UK nationality law is that most of those born in Northern Ireland are UK nationals, whether or not they so choose. Renunciation of British citizenship requires the payment of a fee, currently £372.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In recent censuses, residents said they held the following passports:<sup id="cite_ref-2011_Census_data_156-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2011_Census_data-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-passport_held_2021_170-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-passport_held_2021-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<table class="wikitable sortable" style="text-align:right">

</th>

</th></tr>

<td>52.6%
</td></tr>

</td></tr>
